ble-doc selinuxon_playbook.yml
> MODULE ansible.posix.selinux (/home/ansible/ansible/10_selinux/collections/ansible_collections/ansible/posix/plugins/modules/selinux.py)

  Configures the SELinux mode and policy.
  A reboot may be required after usage.
  Ansible will not issue this reboot but will let you know when it is required.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   configfile  The path to the SELinux configuration file, if non-standard.
        aliases: [conf, file]
        default: /etc/selinux/config
        type: str

   policy  The name of the SELinux policy to use (e.g. `targeted') will be required unless `state=disabled'.
        default: null
        type: str

   state   The SELinux mode.
        choices: [disabled, enforcing, permissive]
        type: str

   update_kernel_param  If set to `true', will update also the kernel boot parameters when disabling/enabling SELinux.
                        The `grubby' tool must be present on the target system for this to work.
        default: false
        type: bool

REQUIREMENTS:  libselinux-python


AUTHOR: Derek Carter (@goozbach) <goozbach@friocorte.com>

EXAMPLES:
- name: Enable SELinux
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: enforcing

- name: Put SELinux in permissive mode, logging actions that would be blocked.
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: permissive

>EMODULE ansible.posix.selinux (/home/ansible/ansible/10_selinux/collections/ansible_collections/ansible/posix/plugins/modules/selinux.py)

  Configures the SELinux mode and policy.
  A reboot may be required after usage.
  Ansible will not issue this reboot but will let you know when it is required.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   configfile  The path to the SELinux configuration file, if non-standard.
        aliases: [conf, file]
        default: /etc/selinux/config
        type: str

   policy  The name of the SELinux policy to use (e.g. `targeted') will be required unless `state=disabled'.
        default: null
        type: str

   state   The SELinux mode.
        choices: [disabled, enforcing, permissive]
        type: str

   update_kernel_param  If set to `true', will update also the kernel boot parameters when disabling/enabling SELinux.
                        The `grubby' tool must be present on the target system for this to work.
        default: false
        type: bool

REQUIREMENTS:  libselinux-python


AUTHOR: Derek Carter (@goozbach) <goozbach@friocorte.com>

EXAMPLES:
- name: Enable SELinux
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: enforcing

- name: Put SELinux in permissive mode, logging actions that would be blocked.
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: permissive

> MODULE ansible.posix.selinux (/home/ansible/ansible/10_selinux/collections/ansible_collections/ansible/posix/plugins/modules/selinux.py)

  Configures the SELinux mode and policy.
  A reboot may be required after usage.
  Ansible will not issue this reboot but will let you know when it is required.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   configfile  The path to the SELinux configuration file, if non-standard.
        aliases: [conf, file]
        default: /etc/selinux/config
        type: str

   policy  The name of the SELinux policy to use (e.g. `targeted') will be required unless `state=disabled'.
        default: null
        type: str

   state   The SELinux mode.
        choices: [disabled, enforcing, permissive]
        type: str

   update_kernel_param  If set to `true', will update also the kernel boot parameters when disabling/enabling SELinux.
                        The `grubby' tool must be present on the target system for this to work.
        default: false
        type: bool

REQUIREMENTS:  libselinux-python


AUTHOR: Derek Carter (@goozbach) <goozbach@friocorte.com>

EXAMPLES:
- name: Enable SELinux
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: enforcing

- name: Put SELinux in permissive mode, logging actions that would be blocked.
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    policy: targeted
    state: permissive

- name: Disable SELinux
  ansible.posix.selinux:
    state: disabled

RETURN VALUES:

   configfile  Path to SELinux configuration file.
        returned: always
        sample: /etc/selinux/config
        type: str

   msg     Messages that describe changes that were made.
        returned: always
        sample: Config SELinux state changed from 'disabled' to 'permissive'
        type: str

   policy  Name of the SELinux policy.
        returned: always
        sample: targeted
        type: str

   reboot_required  Whether or not an reboot is required for the changes to take effect.
        returned: always
        sample: true
        type: bool

   state   SELinux mode.
        returned: always
        sample: enforcing
        type: str

[ansible@ansible ~/ansible/10_selinux]$ # (ansible)
sudo dnf install -y httpd mod_ssl

cd /home/ansible/ansible/10_selinux/
mkdir -p files
cp /etc/httpd/conf/httpd.conf files/httpd.conf
sed -i 's;^DocumentRoot "/var/www/html";DocumentRoot "/www";' files/httpd.conf
sed -i 's;^<Directory "/var/www/html">;<Directory "/www">;' files/httpd.conf
sed -i 's;^Listen 80;Listen 8888;' files/httpd.conf
echo "<h1><center>webservers SELinux configuration</center></h1>" > files/index.html

e-doc sefcontext
> MODULE community.general.sefcontext (/usr/share/ansible/collections/ansible_collections/community/general/plugins/modules/sefcontext.py)

  Manages SELinux file context mapping definitions.
  Similar to the `semanage fcontext' command.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   ftype   The file type that should have SELinux contexts applied.
           The following file type options are available:
           `a' for all files,
           `b' for block devices,
           `c' for character devices,
           `d' for directories,
           `f' for regular files,
           `l' for symbolic links,
           `p' for named pipes,
           `s' for socket files.
        choices: [a, b, c, d, f, l, p, s]
        default: a
        type: str

   ignore_selinux_state  Useful for scenarios (chrooted environment) that you cannot get the real SELinux state.
        default: false
        type: bool

   reload  Reload SELinux policy after commit.
           Note that this does not apply SELinux file contexts to existing files.
        default: true
        type: bool

   selevel  SELinux range for the specified `target'.
            Defaults to `s0' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
        aliases: [serange]
        default: null
        type: str

   setype  SELinux type for the specified `target'.
        default: null
        type: str

   seuser  SELinux user for the specified `target'.
           Defaults to `system_u' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
>EMODULE community.general.sefcontext (/usr/share/ansible/collections/ansible_collections/community/general/plugins/modules/sefcontext.py)

  Manages SELinux file context mapping definitions.
  Similar to the `semanage fcontext' command.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   ftype   The file type that should have SELinux contexts applied.
           The following file type options are available:
           `a' for all files,
           `b' for block devices,
           `c' for character devices,
           `d' for directories,
           `f' for regular files,
           `l' for symbolic links,
           `p' for named pipes,
           `s' for socket files.
        choices: [a, b, c, d, f, l, p, s]
        default: a
        type: str

   ignore_selinux_state  Useful for scenarios (chrooted environment) that you cannot get the real SELinux state.
        default: false
        type: bool

   reload  Reload SELinux policy after commit.
           Note that this does not apply SELinux file contexts to existing files.
        default: true
        type: bool

   selevel  SELinux range for the specified `target'.
            Defaults to `s0' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
        aliases: [serange]
        default: null
        type: str

   setype  SELinux type for the specified `target'.
        default: null
        type: str

   seuser  SELinux user for the specified `target'.
           Defaults to `system_u' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
> MODULE community.general.sefcontext (/usr/share/ansible/collections/ansible_collections/community/general/plugins/modules/sefcontext.py)

  Manages SELinux file context mapping definitions.
  Similar to the `semanage fcontext' command.

OPTIONS (red indicates it is required):

   ftype   The file type that should have SELinux contexts applied.
           The following file type options are available:
           `a' for all files,
           `b' for block devices,
           `c' for character devices,
           `d' for directories,
           `f' for regular files,
           `l' for symbolic links,
           `p' for named pipes,
           `s' for socket files.
        choices: [a, b, c, d, f, l, p, s]
        default: a
        type: str

   ignore_selinux_state  Useful for scenarios (chrooted environment) that you cannot get the real SELinux state.
        default: false
        type: bool

   reload  Reload SELinux policy after commit.
           Note that this does not apply SELinux file contexts to existing files.
        default: true
        type: bool

   selevel  SELinux range for the specified `target'.
            Defaults to `s0' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
        aliases: [serange]
        default: null
        type: str

   setype  SELinux type for the specified `target'.
        default: null
        type: str

   seuser  SELinux user for the specified `target'.
           Defaults to `system_u' for new file contexts and to existing value when modifying file contexts.
...skipping...
EXAMPLES:
- name: Allow apache to modify files in /srv/git_repos
  community.general.sefcontext:
    target: '/srv/git_repos(/.*)?'
    setype: httpd_sys_rw_content_t
    state: present

- name: Substitute file contexts for path /srv/containers with /var/lib/containers
  community.general.sefcontext:
    target: /srv/containers
    substitute: /var/lib/containers
    state: present

- name: Delete file context path substitution for /srv/containers
  community.general.sefcontext:
    target: /srv/containers
    substitute: /var/lib/containers
    state: absent

- name: Delete any file context mappings for path /srv/git
  community.general.sefcontext:
    target: /srv/git
    state: absent

- name: Apply new SELinux file context to filesystem
  ansible.builtin.command: restorecon -irv /srv/git_repos

[ansible@ansible ~/ansible/10_selinux]$ cat pre_playbook.yml
---
- name: SELinux 관리
  hosts: webservers
  become: true
  tasks:
    - name: SELinux 모드 설정
      ansible.posix.selinux:
        policy: targeted
        state: enforcing

    - name: WEB 패키지 설치
      ansible.builtin.dnf:
        name: httpd
        state: present

    - name: WEB 서비스 설정 - httpd.conf
      ansible.builtin.copy:
        src: /home/ansible/10_selinux/files/httpd.conf
        dest: /etc/httpd/conf/httpd.conf
        owner: root
        group: root
        mode: '0644'

    - name: WEB 디렉토리 생성 - /www
      ansible.builtin.file:
        path: /www
        state: directory

    - name: WEB 서비스 설정 - index.html
      ansible.builtin.copy:
        src: /home/ansible/10_selinux/files/index.html
        dest: /www/index.html
        owner: root
        group: root
        mode: '0644'

    - name: WEB 서비스가 네트워크 연결 가능하도록 boolean 설정
      ansible.posix.seboolean:
        name: httpd_can_network_connect
        state: true
        persistent: true

    - name: 웹루트 디렉토리 fcontext 설정
      community.general.sefcontext:
      #ansible.posix.sefcontext:
        target: '/www(/.*)?'
        setype: httpd_sys_content_t
        state: present

    - name: 웹루트 디렉토리 fcontext 적용
      ansible.builtin.command:
        cmd: restorecon -Rv /www

    - name: SELinux Port 설정
      community.general.seport:
      #ansible.posix.seport:
        ports: 8888
        proto: tcp
        setype: http_port_t
        state: present

    - name: WEB 서비스 시작 및 부팅 시 자동 시작 설정
      ansible.builtin.systemd:
        name: httpd
        state: started

    - name: 방화벽 설정 - http 서비스 허용
      ansible.posix.firewalld:
        port: 8888/tcp
        permanent: true
        state: enabled
        immediate: true
